Bhājanasthala, Svaparājñasthala, 8. and 9. Jñānaśūnyasthala. The Characteristics of these are told in due order. Please listen, O mendicant. (3-5)
मुख्यार्थो लक्षणार्थश्च यत्र नास्ति चिदात्मनि । विशृङ्खलतया तस्य प्रसादः स्वीकृतो भवेत् ।। ६ ।।
In the Soul made up of "Cit" (supreme intelligence or consciousness), there is neither the Primary sense nor the Indicated sense; owing to its absolute freedom, the Soul (Śivayogin) enjoys the bliss of "Prasādā", the most profound grace in the form of Self-realisation. (6)
मातृमेयप्रमाणादिव्यवहारे विहारिणीम् । संवित्साक्षात्कृतिं लब्ध्वा योगी स्वात्मनि तिष्ठति ।। ७ ।।
Having obtained the realisation of the consciousness (knowledge or awareness) the consciousness which is present in the process of the knower, the known, the knowledge, etc., - the Śivayogin remains in his own Self. (7)
अद्वैतबोधनिर्धूतभेदावेशस्य योगिनः । साक्षात्कृतमहासंवित्प्रकाशस्य क्व बन्धनम् ।।८ ।।
In the case of the Śivayogin in whom the influence of difference has been eradicated by the knowledge of non-duality and who has the revelation of the great brilliance of
supreme consciousness, whence can there be bondage at all? (8)
चिदात्मनि शिवे न्यस्तं जगदेतच्चराचरम् । जायते तन्मयं सर्वमग्नौ काष्ठदिकं यथा ।। ९ ।।
This entire world of the movable and the immovable which is placed in Śiva consisting in pure consciousness, becomes Śiva in form just as the wooden sticks, etc., assume the form of fire in fire. (9)
न भाति पृथ्वी न जलं न तेजो नैव मारुतः । नाकाशो न परं तत्त्वं शिवे दृष्टे चिदात्मनि ।।१०।।
When once Śiva, who is of the nature of consciousness, is seen, earth does not appear, nor water, nor light, nor wind, nor ether nor any other supreme principle. (10)
ज्योतिर्लिङ्गे चिदाकारे ज्वलत्यन्तर्निरन्तरम् । विलीनं निखिलं तत्त्वं पश्यन् योगी न लिप्यते । । १ १ ।।
Visualing steadily inside the entire multitude of principles(36) as having merged in the "Jyotirlinga" which is in the form of consciounsess and which shines inside, the Śivayogin is not contaminated by anything. (11)
अन्तर्मुखेन मनसा स्वात्मज्योतिषि चिन्मये । सर्वानप्यर्थविषयान् जुह्वन् योगी प्रमोदते ।।१२।।
Offering as oblation all the concepts of the objects, through his inward mind, into the fire of his Self (which is Paraśiva), which is of the nature of consciouness, the Śivayogin gets delighted. (12)
सच्चिदानन्दजलधौ शिवे स्वात्मनि निर्मलः । समर्प्य सकलान् भुङ्क्ते विषयान् तत्प्रसादतः । । १ ३ ।।
After offering all the objects of the senses to his own

--- Page 156 ---
A preamble to Śri Siddhāntaśikhāmaņi
pristine Self which is Paraśiva, the ocean of existence, intelligence, and bliss, the Śivayogin who is pure, partakes them as the "Prasāda" of that Śiva. (13)
प्रकाशते या सर्वेषां माया सैवौदनाकृतिः । लीयते तत्र चिल्लिङ्गे शिष्टं तत्परिकीर्तितम् ।।१४।।
जगदङ्गे परिग्रस्ते मायापाशविजृम्भिते । स्वात्मज्योतिषि बोधेन तदेकमवशिष्यते । । १ ५ ।।
That "Māyā" (evolved existence), which appears to all, is in the form of the food (odana); it merges into that "Cillinga" (Linga in the form of intelligence or consciousness); this is called "Śista" (remnant) for the Śivayogin. (14) When the body, in the form of the world, which is bound by the fetters of Māyā, is overwhelmed through enlightenment, by the brightness of Self-awareness, what remains is that "Māyātattva" only. (15)
अखण्डसच्चिदानन्दपरब्रह्मस्वरूपिणः । जीवन्मुक्तस्य धीरस्य माया कैङ्कर्यवादिनी ।। १६ ।।
To the Śivayogin who is of the nature of Parabrahman characterised by absolute existence, intelligence and bliss, who is liberated even while alive and who is enlightened, Māyā acts as the maid-servent. (16)
विश्वसंमोहिनी माया बहुशक्तिनिरङ्‌कुशा। शिवैकत्वमुपेतस्य न पुरः स्थातुमीहति ।।१७।।
The Māyā who fascinates the world and who is independent through her abundent power, does not like to stand before the Śivayogin who has attained oneness with Śiva. (17)
ज्योतिर्लिङ्गे चिदाकारे निमग्नेन महात्मना। भुज्यमाना यथायोगं नश्यन्ति विषयाः स्वतः ।। १८ ।।
The objects of senses which are being enjoyed as the occasion arises by the great Śivayogin who has merged in the "Joytirilinga" of the nature of consciousness, get themselves exhausted. (18)
शब्दादयोऽपि विषया भुज्यमानास्तदिन्द्रियैः । आत्मन्येव विलीयन्ते सरितः सागरे यथा ।।१ ९ ।।
The objects of senses such as sound, etc., being enjoyed by their respective senses, get absorbed in the Atman himself like the rivers in the ocean. (19)
अर्थजातमशेषं तु ग्रसन् योगी प्रशाम्यति । स्वात्मनैवास्थितो भानुस्तेजोजालमशेषतः ।।२०।।
The Śivayogin who has absorbed into his Self the entire multitude of sense-objects, becomes calm, like the sun who remains by withdrawing the entire net-work of his rays in himself. (20)
लिङ्गैक्ये तु समापन्ने चरणाचरणे गते। निर्देही स भवेद्योगी चराचरविनाशकः ।। २१ ।।
When the universe consisting of the movable and the immovable objects (caraņa-acaraņa-cara-acara), becomes absorbed into the "Linga", the Śivayogin who is free from all bodily characteristics, comes to be called as "Carācaravināśaka" (one who causes the universe to disappear). (21)
अनाद्यविद्यामूला हि प्रतीतिर्जगतामियम् । स्वात्मैकबोधात्तन्नाशे कुतो विश्वप्रकाशनम् ।। २२ ।।
This apprehension of the world is rooted in the beginningless nescience. When that is lost due to the realisation of non-duality of Atman, whence can there be the flash of the world? (22)

--- Page 157 ---
A preamble to Śri Siddhāntaśikhāmaņi
यथा मेघाः समुद्भूता विलीयन्ते नभस्थले । तथात्मनि विलीयन्ते विषयाः स्वानुभाविनः ।। २३ ।।
Just as the cloud arising in the expanse of the sky gets absorbed there itself, so are the objects of the senses get absorbed in the Self of the Śivayogin who is accomplished in the mystic experience of the Self as Śiva himself. (23)
स्वप्ने दृष्टं यथा वस्तु प्रबोधे लयमश्नुते । तथा सांसारिकं सर्वमात्मज्ञाने विनश्यति ।। २४ ।।
Just as the object seen in the dream disappears on awakening, so does all that is mundane disappear on the dawn of Self-realisation. (24)
जाग्रत्स्वप्नसुषुप्तिभ्यः परावस्थामुपेयुषः । किं वा प्रमाणं किं ज्ञेयं किं वा ज्ञानस्य साधनम् ।। २५ ।।
In the case of the Śivayogin who has attained to the supreme state (turīyāvasthā) beyond the wakeful, dream and dreamless sleep states, what is the means of knowledge? What is to be known? What is the instrument of knowledge? (25)
तुर्यातीतपदं यत्तद् दूरं वाङ्मनसाध्वनः । अनुप्रविश्य तद्योगी न भूयो विश्वमीक्षते ।। २६ ।।
The state of "Turyātīta" (the state beyond the fourth state) is that which is beyond the range of speech and mind. Having entered that state, the Yogin does not witness the world again. (26)
नान्यत् पश्यति योगीन्द्रो नान्यज्जानाति किञ्चन । नान्यच्छृणोति सन्दृष्टे चिदानन्दमये शिवे ।। २७।।
When Paraśiva, who is consisting of intelligence and bliss, is seen (realised), the great Śivayogin, does not see anything else, does not know anything else and does not hear
anything else.(27) असदेव जगत्सर्वं सदिव प्रतिभासते । ज्ञाते शिवे तदज्ञानं स्वरूपमुपपद्यते ।। २८ ।।
The entire world, which is "non-existent" apart from Paraśiva, appears as "existent" apart from Paraśiva. When Śiva is realised, that ignorance transforms into the form of
"Cicchakti" (Jñānaśakti). (28) ब्रह्माण्डशतकोटीनां सर्गस्थितिलयान् प्रति । स्थानभूतो विमर्शो यस्तद्भाण्डस्थलमुच्यते ।। २९ ।।
The "Vimarśā-sakti" (Power of discrimination) of the Śivayogin which is the basis of creation, protection and absorption of hundreds of crores of worlds, is said to be "Bhānda-sthala" (realisation of the substratum). (29)
विमर्शाख्या पराशक्तिर्विश्वोद्धासनकारिणी। साक्षिणी सर्वभूतानां समिन्धे सर्वतोमुखी ।। ३० ।।
The "Parāśakti" called "Vimarśā", who is the revealer of the entire world and who is the witness of all the beings, displays herself all around. (30)
विश्वं यत्र लयं याति विभात्यात्मा चिदाकृतिः । सदानन्दमयः साक्षात् सा विमर्शमयी कला ।। ३१ ।।
She in whom the world gets absorbed, who appears as the Atman in the form of consciousness and in whom the Yogin actually enjoys the state of reality and bliss, is the Śakti (Kala) of the nature of "Vimarśā". (31)
पराहन्तासमावेशपरिपूर्णविमर्शवान् । सर्वज्ञः सर्वगः साक्षी सर्वकर्ता महेश्वरः ।। ३२ ।।
The Śivayogin who is endowed with absolute "Vimarśā" inspired by the conception of his "Self" being Śiva

--- Page 158 ---
A preamble to Śri Siddhāntaśikhāmaņi
(Parahantā) is the omniscient, all-pervasive, all-witnessing and omnipotent Maheśvara (the Great Lord Śiva). (32) विश्वाधारमहासंवित्प्रकाशपरिपूरितम् ।
पराहन्तामयं प्राहुर्विमर्श परमात्मनः ।। ३३ ।।
विमर्शभाण्डविन्यस्तविश्वतत्त्वविजृम्भणः । अनन्यमुखसम्प्रेक्षी मुक्तः स्वात्मनि तिष्ठति ।। ३४ ।।
The "Vimarsa" of the Paramatman which is filled fully with the brilliance of the great knowledge (consciousness) that happens to be the substratum of the world, is said to be filled with "Parāhantā". (33) Having deposited the displayed Principles forming the the universe in the receptacle of his "Vimarśā", the Śivayogin stations himself in his own Self (which is Śiva) without being inclined to see anything else. (34)
समस्तजगदण्डानां सर्गस्थित्यन्तकारणम् । विमर्शो भासते यत्र तद्भाजनमिहोच्यते ।। ३५ ।।
विमर्शाख्या पराशक्तिर्विश्ववैचित्र्यकारिणी। यस्मिन् प्रतिष्ठितं ब्रह्म तदिदं विश्वभाजनम् ।। ३६ ।।
That in which the "Vimarśaśakti" that is the cause for the creation, protection and absorption of the entire multitude of worlds, appears, is said to be the "Bhajana" here (in the case of the Śivayogin). (35) That in which Parāśakti called "Vimarśa" that causes the variety of the world, is established, is the Paraśivabrahman and that is the "Bhājana" (receptacle) of the world. (36)
अन्तःकरणरूपेण जगदङ्कुररूपतः । यस्मिन् विभाति चिच्छक्ति ब्रह्मभूतः स उच्यते ।। ३७ ।।
He in whom the "Cicchakti" (power of knowledge)
appears in the form of his consciousness and in the form of the sport of the universe, is said to be Paraśivabrahman. (37) यथा चन्द्रे स्थिरा ज्योत्स्ना विश्ववस्तुप्रकाशिनी । तथा शक्तिविमर्शात्मा प्रकारे ब्रह्मणि स्थिता ।। ३८ ।।
Just as moonlight which reveals all the objects, is permanent in the moon, so is the Vimarśāśakti permanent in Brahman (Paraśiva) in the aspect created for it by his will. (38)
अकारः शिव आख्यातो हकारः शक्तिरुच्यते । शिवशक्तिमयं ब्रह्म स्थितमेकमहंपदे ।। ३९ ।।
"Akāra" (the syllable "अ") is said to be Śiva and "Hakāra" (the syllable "ह") is said to be Śakti. Śiva-Śakti-synthesis is Brahman. It stands as one denoted by the word "Aham". (39)
अहन्तां परमां प्राप्य शिवशक्तिमयीं स्थिराम् । ब्रह्मभूयंगतो योगी विश्वात्मा प्रतिभासते ।।४० ।।
Having attained that supreme "Ahantā" consisting in the permanent Śiva-Śakti-synthesis, the Yogin who has become Paraśiva-Śakti-synthesis, appears as the universe in form. (40)
वृक्षस्थं पत्रापुष्पादि वटबीजस्थितं यथा। तथा हृदयबीजस्थं विश्वमेतत् परात्मनः ।।४१।।
Just as the leaves, flowers, etc., of the (banyan) tree, are in their potential form hidden in the seed of the banyan tree, so is the entire universe hidden in the seed of the heart of the Paramātman.(41)
दिक्कालाद्यनवच्छिन्नं चिदानन्दमयं महत् । यस्य रूपमिदं ख्यातं सोऽङ्गालेप इहोज्यते ।। ४२ ।।

--- Page 159 ---
A preamble to Śri Siddhāntaśikhāmaņi
He (the Śivayogin) whose form is well known to be not subjected to the limitations of place, time, etc., to be consisting in supreme intelligence and bliss and to be magnamimous, is here called "Angālepa" (one whose body is not the means of defilement to him). (42)
समस्तजगदात्मापि संविद्रूपो महामतिः । लिप्यते नैव संसारैर्यथा धूमादिभिर्नभः ।।४३ ।।
The enlightened Yogin who is the "Atman" of the entire universe and who is of the nature of consciousness, is not at all stained by the worldly associations, like the sky by the smoke, etc. (43)
न विधिर्न निषेधश्च न विकल्पो न वासना। केवलं चित्स्वरूपस्य गलितप्राकृतात्मनः ।।४४ ।।
There is neither injunction nor prohibition, neither indecision nor mental impression in the case of the Śivayogin who is of the nature of pure consciousness and who has been relieved of the ordinary inclinations to action. (44)
घटादिषु पृथग्भूतं यथाऽऽकाशं न भिद्यते । तथोपाधिगतं ब्रह्म नानारूपं न भिद्यते ।। ४५ ।।
अनश्वरमनिर्देश्यं यथा व्योम प्रकाशते । तथा ब्रह्मापि चैतन्यमत्र वैशेषिकी कला ।।४६ ।।
Just as the ether which is separated as contained in the pot, etc., does not differ, so the Brahman who has many forms as contained in the adjuncts does not differ.(45) Just as the ether appears as not transitory and as indescribable, so does Brahman appear. But consciousness has been his distingnishing mark.(46) न देवत्वं न मानुष्यं न तिर्यक्तवं न चान्यथा।
सर्वाकारत्वमाख्यातं जीवन्मुक्तस्य योगिनः ।।४७ ।।
There is no form of a god, nor of a man, nor of an animal, nor of any other thing in the case of the Śivayogin who is liberated even while alive. He is said to be omniform. (47)
अप्रमेये चिदाकारे ब्रह्मण्यद्वैतवैभवे । विलीनः किं नु जानाति स्वात्मानं परमेव वा ।।४८ ।।
When the Śivayogin is completely merged into the splendour of non-duality in Paraśiva who is beyond grasp and who is of the form of supreme consciousness, can he know of his "Self" or of the "other Self"? (48)
यत्र नास्ति भिदायोगादहं त्वमिति विभ्रमः । न संयोगो वियोगश्च न ज्ञेयज्ञातृकल्पना ।।४९ ।।
न बन्धो न च मुक्तिश्च न देवाद्यभिमानिता। न सुखं नैव दुःखं च नाज्ञानं ज्ञानमेव वा ।। ५० ।।
नोत्कृष्टत्वं न हीनत्वं नोपरिष्टान्न चाप्यधः । न पश्चान्नैव पुरतो न दूरे किञ्चिदन्तरे ।। ५१ ।।
सर्वाकारे चिदानन्दे सत्यरूपिणि शाश्वते । पराकाशमये तस्मिन् परे ब्रह्मणि निर्मले ।। ५२ ।।
एकीभावमुपेतानां योगिनां परमात्मनाम् । परापरपरिज्ञानपरिहासकथा कुतः ।।५३ ।।
There is no delusion of difference as regards "I" and "You" due to communion, neither conjuction nor separation, nor any conception of the knowable and the knower, there is neither bondage nor release, nor any conceit as god, etc., neither joy nor sorrow, neither

--- Page 160 ---
A preamble to Śri Siddhāntaśikhāmaņi
ignorance nor knowledge; there is neither excellence nor degradation, neither above nor below, neither behind nor in front, neither far nor near; such is Paraśiva who is omniform, who is the bliss of consciousness, who is eternally true, who is permanent, who is of the nature of the supreme ether and who is pure; in the case of the great Souls, the Śivayogins, who have become one with such Paraśiva, how can there be the ridicule in the form of the awareness of one's self and the otherself? (49-53)
देशकालानवच्छिन्नतेजोरूपसमाश्रयात् । स्वपरज्ञानविरहात् स्वपराज्ञस्थलं विदुः ।।५४ ।।
Due to his absorption in the brilliance of Paraśiva which is free from spacio-temporal limitations and due to his lack of awareness of his "Self" and another "Self", this state of the Śivayogin is known as "Śvaparājñasthala".(54)
त्वन्ताहन्ताविनिर्मुक्ते शून्यकल्पे चिदम्बरे। एकीभूतस्य सिद्धस्य भावाभावकथा कुतः ।। ५५ ।।
In the case of the accomplished Śivayogin who has become one with the Ether of Consciousness (Paraśiva) which is free from the notions of "You" and "I" and which is thus a state similar to void, how can there be the conception of the positive and the negative? (55)
अहंभावस्य शून्यत्वादभावस्य तथात्मनः । भावभावविनिर्मुक्तो जीवन्मुक्तः प्रकाशते ।। ५६ ।।
सुखदुःखादिभावेषु नाभावो भाव एव वा । विद्यते चित्स्वरूपस्य निर्लेपस्य महात्मनः ।।५७ ।।
The Śivayogin who is liberated even while alive appears as relieved from the "Bhava" and the "Abhāva", because of the absence of the positive conception of "I" and of the negative conception that there is no Atman (apart from the body). (56) In the case of the Śivayogin who is of the nature of supreme consciousness and who is not associated with anything, there is no conception of either "Abhāva" or "bhava" (57)

यस्मिन् ज्योतिषि चिद्रूपे दृश्यते नैव किञ्चन। सद्रूपं वाप्यसद्रूपं भावाभावं विमुञ्चतः ।।५८।।
To the luminous, consciousness-form Śivayogī who has abandoned existence and non-existence, that is, who has gone beyond this duality, neither the existent form nor the non-existent form is seen at all. ।।५८।।

प्रतीयमानौ विद्येते भावभावौ न कुत्रचित्। लिङ्गक्ये सति यत्तस्माद्भावाभावलयस्थलम् ।।५९ ।।
Since the manifest existence and non-existence do not exist anywhere for the Śivayogī who has attained oneness, that is, harmony, with the Śivaliṅga, that state is therefore called Bhāvābhāva-laya-sthala, the stage of dissolution of existence and non-existence. ।।५९।।

ज्ञानशून्यस्थल (१०१) परापरसमापेक्षभावाभावविवेचनम् ज्ञानं ब्रह्मणि तन्नास्ति ज्ञानशून्यस्थलं विदुः ।।६०।।
The knowledge consisting in the discrimination of existence and non-existence in relation to the higher and lower does not remain in Brahman, that is, in the Śivayogī who is of the nature of Parabrahman. Therefore, the learned call this the Jñānaśūnya-sthala, the stage devoid of such knowledge. ॥६०॥

जले जलमिव न्यस्तं वह्नौ वह्निरिवार्पितम्। परे ब्रह्मणि लीनात्मा विभागेन न दृश्यते ।। ६१ ।।
Just as water placed in water and fire placed in fire are no longer distinguishable, the Śivayogī whose self has merged into Parabrahman is not seen as separate. ।।६१।।

सर्वात्मनि परे तत्त्वे भेदशङ्काविवर्जिते। ज्ञात्रादिव्यवहारोत्थं कुतो ज्ञानं विभाव्यते ।। ६२ ।।
In the supreme Reality, which is the Self of all and is completely free from the possibility of distinction, how can knowledge arising from the activity of the knower, the known, and knowledge itself be experienced? ॥६२॥


निर्विकारं निराकारं नित्यं सीमाविवर्जितम्। व्योमवत् परमं ब्रह्म निर्विकल्पतया स्थितम् ।।६३ ।।
The supreme Brahman, free from modifications, formless, eternal, and without limits, exists in a non-conceptual state, all-pervading like space.

न पृथ्व्यादीनि भूतानि न ग्रहा नैव तारकाः। न देवा न मनुष्याश्च न तिर्यचो न चापरे ।।६४।।
The five great elements beginning with earth, the planets such as the moon and sun, the stars such as Aśvinī and others, the gods, human beings, birds, and all other beings are not perceived.

तस्मिन् केवलचिन्मात्रसत्तानन्दैकलक्षणे। त्वन्ताहन्तादिसंरूढं विज्ञानं केन भाव्यते ।। ६५।।
In that Brahman, whose sole nature is pure Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss, who can experience the firmly established knowledge of ‘thou’ and ‘I’ and the like, that is, the knowledge of distinction? In other words, no one can.

ज्ञेयाभावाद्विशेषेण शून्यकल्पं विभाव्यते। ज्ञातुज्ञेयादिभिः शून्यं शून्यं ज्ञानादिभिर्गुणैः ।। ६६ ।।
Because of the complete absence of the object of knowledge, it is experienced as resembling emptiness. This experience is devoid of the knower, knowledge, the known, and the like, and is devoid of qualities such as knowledge, desire, and the like.

आदावन्ते च मध्ये च शून्यं सर्वत्र सर्वदा। द्वितीयेन पदार्थेन शून्यं शून्यं विभाव्यते ।। ६७।।
It is empty at the beginning, in the middle, and at the end; it is empty everywhere and always. This emptiness is experienced as being devoid of any second entity apart from Brahman.

केवलं सच्चिदानन्दप्रकाशाद्वयलक्षणम् । शून्यकल्पं पराकाशं परब्रह्म प्रकाशते ।। ६८ ।।
The Parabrahman, whose sole nature is the light of Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss and non-duality, and which is like the supreme space resembling emptiness, shines forth.

शून्यज्ञानादिसङ्कल्पे शून्यसर्वार्थसाधने। ज्योतिर्लिङ्ग चिदाकारे स्वप्रकाशे निरुत्तरे ।। एकीभावमुपेतस्य कथं ज्ञानस्य सम्भवः ।।६९।।
For one who has become one with the Jyotirliṅga, which is consciousness-form, self-luminous, supreme, devoid of concepts such as knowledge, and devoid of all means of attaining the aims of life, how can knowledge arise? For he has himself become of the nature of knowledge.

यस्य कार्यदशा नास्ति कारणत्वमथापि वा। शेषत्वं नैव शेषित्वं स मुक्तः पर उच्यते ।। ७० ।।
He who has neither the state of effect nor the state of cause, who is neither the remainder, that is, the subordinate limb, nor the possessor of the remainder, that is, the whole or principal, is called the supreme liberated one. ।।७०॥

शिवैक्यस्थल

एतावदुक्त्वा परमप्रबोधमद्वैतमानन्दशिवप्रकाशम् । देव्यै पुरा भाषितमीश्वरेण तूष्णीमभूद् ध्यानपरो गणेन्द्रः ।।७१।।
Having thus imparted the supreme awakening, the non-dual revelation of blissful Śiva, which had formerly been secretly taught by Īśvara to the Goddess, Gaṇendra Reṇukācārya became silent and absorbed in meditation. ।।७१।।

एवमुक्त्वा समासीनं शिवयोगपरायणम् । रेणुकं तं समालोक्य बभाषे प्राञ्जलिर्मुनिः ।।७२ ।।
Having said this, Agastya Muni, beholding Reṇukācārya, who was seated and wholly devoted to Śivayoga, spoke with folded hands. ।।७२।।

शिवयोगविशेषज्ञ शिवज्ञानमहोदधे। समस्तवेदशास्त्रादिव्यवहारधुरन्धर ।।७३।।
O knower of the mysteries of Śivayoga! O great ocean of Śivajñāna! O master of the practical knowledge of all the Vedas, Śāstras, and other scriptures! ।।७३।।

आलोकमात्रनिर्भूतसर्वसंसारबन्धन स्वच्छन्दचरितोल्लास स्वप्रकाशात्मवच्छिव।।७४।।
O Śiva, by whose mere radiance all the bonds of worldly existence are destroyed, who rejoices in spontaneous conduct, and who is self-luminous like the Self! ।।७४।।

अवतीर्णमिदं शास्त्रमनवहां त्वदाननात्। श्रुत्वा मे मोदते चित्तं ज्योतिः पश्ये शिवाभिधम् ।। ७५ ।।
Having heard this pure Śāstra, which has emerged from your mouth, my mind is filled with joy. I behold the light called Śiva. ।।७५।।

अद्य मे सफलं जन्म गतो मे चित्तविभ्रमः। सनाता पाशविच्छित्तस्तपांसि फलितानि च।।७६ ।।
Today my birth has become fruitful. The delusion of my mind has disappeared. The bonds have been severed, and my austerities have borne fruit. ।।७६।।

इदानीमेव मे जातं मुनिराजोत्तमोत्तमम् । इतः परं मया नास्ति सदृशो भुरनत्रये ।। ७७ ।।
Only now have I attained the highest excellence among the sages. From this point onward, there is no one equal to me in the three worlds. ।।७७॥

शास्त्रं तव मुखोद्गीर्णं शिवाद्वैतपरम्परम्। मां विना कस्य लोकेषु श्रोतुमस्ति तपः शुभम् ।। ७८ ।।
Who in the world besides me could have the auspicious fortune of austerity to hear the Śāstra of the Śivādvaita tradition that has emerged from your mouth?

तपसां परिपाकेन शङ्करस्य प्रसादतः। आगतस्त्वं महाभाग मां कुतार्थयितुं गिरा ।। ७९ ।।
O greatly blessed one! Through the fruition of my austerities and by the grace of Śaṅkara, you have come to make me fulfilled through your words. ।।७८-७९।।

इति स्तुवन्तं विनयादगस्त्यं मुनिपुङ्गवम् । आलोक्य करुणादृष्ट्या बभाषे स गणेश्वरः ।।८०।।
Beholding the foremost of sages, Agastya, who was thus praising him with humility, Gaṇeśvara Reṇukācārya spoke with a compassionate gaze. ।।८०।।

अगस्त्य मुनिशार्दूल तपः सिद्धमनोरथ। त्वां विना शिवशास्त्रस्य कः श्रोतुमधिकारवान् ।।८१ ।।
O Agastya, foremost among sages, whose aspirations have been fulfilled through austerity! Who besides you is qualified to hear the Śivaśāstra? ।।८१।।

पात्रं शिवप्रसादस्य भवानेको न चापरः। इति निश्चित्य कथितं मया ते तन्त्रमीदृशम् ।।८२।।
You alone are worthy of Śiva's grace, and no one else. Having determined this, I have therefore taught you this Tantra. ।।८२।।

स्थाप्यतां सर्वलोकेषु तन्त्रमेतत् त्वया मुने। ईदृशं शिवबोधस्य साधनं नास्ति कुत्रचित् ।।८३।।
O Muni! Establish and disseminate this Tantra throughout all the worlds. There is nowhere any other means of attaining Śivabodha like this. ।।८३॥

रहस्यमेतत् सर्वज्ञः सर्वानुग्राहकः शिवः। अवादीत् सर्वलोकानां सिद्धये पार्वतीपतिः ।।८४ ।।
The omniscient Śiva, the Lord of Pārvatī and the bestower of grace upon all, revealed this secret for the spiritual accomplishment of all people. ।।८४।।

तदिदं शिवसिद्धान्तसाराणामुत्तमोत्तमम् । वेदवेदान्तसर्वस्वं विद्याचारप्रवर्तकम् ।।८५।।
वीरमाहेश्वरग्राह्वां शिवाद्वैतप्रकाशकम् । परीक्षितेभ्यो दातव्यं शिष्येभ्यो नान्यथा क्वचित् ।।८६।।
This Śāstra is the highest and most excellent among the essences of the Śiva Siddhānta, the very essence of the Veda and Vedānta, the promoter of the conduct of spiritual knowledge, accepted by the Vīra Māheśvaras, and the revealer of Śivādvaita. It should be given only to tested and qualified disciples, and never to others. ।।८५-८६।।

एतच्छ्रवणमात्रेण सर्वेषां पापसंक्षयः। अवतीर्ण मया भूमौ शास्त्रस्यास्य प्रवृत्तये ।। प्रवर्तय शिवाद्वैतं त्वमपि ज्ञानमीदृशम् ।।८७।।
By merely hearing this, the sins of all are destroyed. I have incarnated upon this earth for the propagation of this Śāstra. You too should propagate this knowledge of Śivādvaita in this manner. ॥८७॥

ॐ तत्सत् इति श्रीशिवगीतेषु सिद्धान्तागमेषु शिवाद्वैतविद्यायां शिवयोगशास्त्रे श्रीरेणुकागस्त्यसंवादे वीरशैवधर्मनिर्णये श्रीशिवयोगिशिवाचार्यविरचिते श्रीसिद्धान्तशिखामणौ लिङ्गस्थलान्तर्गत ऐक्यस्थले स्वीकृतप्रसादिस्थलादि-नवविधस्थलप्रसङ्गो नाम विंशतितमः परिच्छेदः ।
Thus, the twentieth chapter of the Śrī Siddhāntaśikhāmaṇi, composed by Śrī Śivayogī Śivācārya, entitled “The exposition of the ninefold stages beginning with the Svīkṛta-prasādi-sthala,” within the Aikya-sthala under the Liṅga-sthala, in the Śivādvaita Vidyā of the Siddhānta Āgamas of the Śrī Śivagītās, the Śivayogaśāstra, and the dialogue between Śrī Reṇukācārya and Agastya in the determination of the Vīraśaiva Dharma, is completed.

इस प्रकार श्रीसिद्धान्तशिखामणि के शिवैक्यस्थल-विषयनवविधलिङ्गप्रसङ्ग नामक विंश परिच्छेद की आचार्य राधेश्यामचतुर्वेदीकृत 'ज्ञानवती' हिन्दी व्याख्या पूर्ण हुई ।।२०।।
Thus concludes the Hindi commentary ‘Jñānavatī’ by Ācārya Rādheśyāma Caturvedī on the twentieth chapter of the Śrī Siddhāntaśikhāmaṇi, entitled “The exposition of the ninefold Liṅga-related stages concerning the Śivāikya-sthala.” ।।२०॥

एकविंशः परिच्छेद

विभीषणाभीष्टदान

इत्युक्त्वा पश्यतस्तस्य पुरस्तादेव रेणुकः। अन्तर्दधे महादेवं चिन्तयन्नन्तरात्मना ।।१ ।।
Having said this, Reṇuka Gaṇeśvara, meditating upon Mahādeva within his inner Self, disappeared from the very presence of Agastya. ।।१॥

य इदं शिवसिद्धान्तं वीरशैवमतं परम् । शृणोति शुद्धमनसा स याति परमां गतिम् ।।२।।
Whoever listens with a pure mind to this Śiva Siddhānta, which accords with the supreme Vīraśaiva doctrine, attains the highest state. ।।२॥

स्वच्छन्दाचाररसिकः स्वेच्छानिर्मितविग्रहः । आससाद पुरीं लङ्कां रेणुको गणनायकः ।।३।।
Reṇuka, the Lord of the Gaṇas, who delights in spontaneous conduct and assumes a body according to his own will, arrived at the city of Laṅkā. ।।३॥

समागतं महाभागं सर्वागमविशारदम् । विभीषणः समालोक्य गेहं प्रवेशयन्निजम् ।।४।।
Seeing the greatly blessed Reṇukācārya, who was proficient in all the divine Āgamas from the Kāmika to the Vātula, having arrived, Vibhīṣaṇa welcomed him into his house. ।।४॥

भद्रासने निजे रम्ये निवेश्य गणनायकम्। अर्घ्यपाद्यादिभिः सर्वैरुपचारैरपूजयत् ।।५ ।।
Having seated the Lord of the Gaṇas upon his beautiful auspicious throne, Vibhīṣaṇa worshipped him with all the customary offerings and services, beginning with arghya and pādya. ।।५॥

पूजितेन प्रसन्नेन रेणुकेन निरूपितः। निषसाद तदभ्याशे स निजासनमाश्रितः ।।६ ।।
Having received the command of Reṇukācārya, who was pleased by the worship offered to him, Vibhīṣaṇa sat near him upon his own seat. ।।६॥

आबभाषे गणेन्द्रं तं कृताञ्जलिर्विभीषणः । मानुषाकारसम्पन्नं साक्षाच्छिवमिवापरम् ।। ७ ।।
Then Vibhīṣaṇa, with folded hands, addressed that Lord of the Gaṇas, who had assumed human form and appeared like another Śiva Himself. ।।७॥

रेणुक त्वं गणाधीश शिवज्ञानपरायण। अवतीर्णं महीमेनामिति सम्यक् श्रुतं मया ।।८।।
O Reṇuka, Lord of the Gaṇas, devoted to Śivajñāna! I have heard clearly that you have incarnated upon this earth. ।।८॥

मद्भाग्यगौरवादद्य समायास्त्वं पुरीमिमाम्। कथं भाग्यविहीनानां सुलभाः स्युर्भवादृशाः ।।९।।
By the greatness of my good fortune, you have come today to this city. How could those who are devoid of good fortune ever have easy access to one such as you? ।।९॥

मत्समो नास्ति लोकेषु भाग्यातिशयवत्तया । यस्य गेहं स्वयं प्राप्तो भवान् साक्षान्महेश्वरः ।।१०।।
There is no one in this world as exceedingly fortunate as I am, for you, who are Mahāśvara Himself, have personally come to my house. ।।१०॥

कृतार्था में पुरी होषा कृतार्थो राक्षसान्वयः। जीवितं च कृतार्थं में यस्य त्वं दृष्टिगोचरः ।।११।।
My city of Laṅkā has become blessed, the lineage of the Rākṣasas has become blessed, and my life has become fulfilled, because you have appeared before my eyes. ।।११॥

इति बुवाणं कल्याणं राक्षसेन्द्रं गणेश्वरः। बभाषे सस्मितो वाणीं विश्वोल्लासकरीं शुभाम् ।।१२।।
To the Lord of the Rākṣasas, who spoke these auspicious words, the Lord of the Gaṇas, with a gentle smile, spoke auspicious words that brought joy to the entire world. ।।१२॥

विभीषण महाभाग जाने त्वां धर्मकोविदम्। त्वां विना कस्य लोकेषु जायते भक्तिरीदृशी ।।१ ३ ।।
O greatly blessed Vibhīṣaṇa! I know you as one who is well-versed in Dharma. In this world, in whom besides you could such devotion arise? ।।१३॥

समस्तशास्त्रसारशं सर्वधर्मपरायणम् । अध्यात्मविद्यानिरतमाहुस्त्वां राक्षसेश्वर ।।१४।।
O Lord of the Rākṣasas! The learned describe you as one who knows the essence of all the Śāstras, who is devoted to all forms of Dharma, and who is deeply engaged in spiritual knowledge. ।।१४॥

त्वदीयधर्मसम्पत्तिं श्रुत्वाहं विस्मिताशयः । व्रजन् कैलासमचलं त्वदन्तिकमुपागतः ।।१५।।
Having heard of your excellence in the practice of Dharma, I, filled with wonder, came to you while proceeding toward the immovable Mount Kailāsa. ।।१५॥

प्रीतोऽस्मि तव चारित्रैः शोभनैर्लोकविश्रुतैः । दास्यामि ते वरं साक्षात् प्रार्थयस्व यथेप्सितम् ।।१६।।
I am pleased with your noble and renowned conduct. I shall personally grant you a boon; ask for whatever you desire. ।।१६॥

इति प्रसादसुमुखे भाषमाणे गणेश्वरे। प्रणम्य परया प्रीत्या व्याजहार विभीषणः ।।१७।।
When the Lord of the Gaṇas, with a gracious and benevolent face, spoke thus, Vibhīṣaṇa bowed down with supreme devotion and said. ।।१७॥

आगमानुग्रहादेव भवतः शिवयोगिनः। दुर्लभाः सर्वलोकानां समपद्यान्त सम्पदः ।।१८।।
By the very grace of the arrival of a Śivayogī such as you, even those attainments that are difficult for all people to obtain are acquired. ।।१८॥

तथापि प्रार्थनीयं मे किञ्चिदस्ति गणेश्वर। सुकृते परिपक्वे हि स्वयं सिद्धयति वाञ्जितम् ।।१९।।
Nevertheless, O Lord of the Gaṇas, I have something to request. When accumulated merit has fully matured, the desired object is accomplished by itself. ।।१९॥

रावणो हि मम भ्राता माहेश्वरशिखामणिः । अदृष्टशत्रुसम्बाधं शशास हि जगत्त्रयम् ।।२०।।
My brother Rāvaṇa, who was the crest-jewel among the devotees of Maheśvara and who had no enemy capable of obstructing him, ruled over the three worlds. ।।२०॥

यस्य प्रतापमतुलं सोढुमक्षमशक्तयः । इन्द्रादयः सुराः सर्वे राज्यलक्ष्मया वियोजिताः ।।२१ ।।
All the gods, including Indra, were unable to withstand his incomparable glory and were deprived of their royal prosperity.

स तु कालवशेनैव स्वचरित्रविपर्ययात् । रणे विष्ण्वतारेण रामेण निहतोऽभवत् ।।२२।।
That mighty Rāvaṇa, however, being subject to the influence of Time and because of the decline of his conduct, was slain in battle by Rāma, the incarnation of Viṣṇu.

स तु रामशराविद्धः कण्ठस्खलितजीवितः । अवशिष्टं समालोक्य मामवादीत् सुदुःखितः ।।२३।।
Pierced through by Rāma’s arrows and with life departing from his throat, Rāvaṇa, seeing me as the sole survivor, spoke to me in great sorrow.

विभीषण विशेषज्ञ महाबुद्धे सुधार्मिक। अवशिष्टोऽसि वंशस्य रक्षसां भाग्यगौरवात् ।।२४ ।।
O Vibhīṣaṇa, O greatly wise and knowledgeable one, devoted to righteous conduct! By the greatness of the fortune of the Rākṣasa lineage, you alone remain as the survivor of our lineage.

वयमज्ञानसम्पन्ना महत्सु द्रोहकारिणः। ईदृशीं तु गतिं प्राप्ता दुस्तरा हि विधिस्थितिः ।।२५ ।।
We, being possessed of ignorance and acting against great and noble beings, have met with such a fate. Indeed, the decree of destiny is difficult to overcome.

नवकं लिङ्ग कोटीनां प्रतिष्ठाप्यमिह स्थले। इति सङ्कल्पितं पूर्वं मया तदवशिष्यते ।। २६ ।।
I had previously resolved to install nine crores of Śivaliṅgas in this city of Laṅkā. Some of them still remain to be installed.

कोटिषट्कं तु लिङ्गानां मया साधु प्रतिष्ठितम्। कोटित्रयं तु लिङ्गानां स्थापनीयमतस्त्वया ।।२७।।
I have properly installed six crores of Śivaliṅgas. Therefore, three crores of Śivaliṅgas remain to be installed by you.

इति तस्य वचः श्रुत्वा दीनबुद्धेमरिष्यतः । तथा साधु करोमीति प्रतिज्ञातं मया तथा ।।२८।।
Hearing the words of that sorrowful and dying Rāvaṇa, I vowed, saying, “I shall indeed accomplish it properly in that manner.”

युगपच्छिवलिङ्गानां कोटित्रयमनुत्तमम् । प्रतिष्ठाप्यं यथाशास्नमिति मे निश्चयोऽभवत् ।।२९ ।।
Then I resolved to install, simultaneously and according to the injunctions of the Śāstras, three crores of excellent Śivaliṅgas.

लिङ्गकोटित्रयस्येह युगपत् स्थापनाविधौ । अविदन्नेकमाचार्य महमेवमवस्थितः ।।३०।।
Since I do not know of a single Ācārya capable of performing the simultaneous installation of these three crores of Śivaliṅgas, I have remained here in Laṅkā in this manner, unable to accomplish the installation.

शिवशास्त्रविशेषज्ञ शिवज्ञाननिधिर्भवान्। आचार्यभावमासाद्य मम पूरय वाञ्छितम् ।।३१।।
You are an expert in the Śivaśāstras and a treasure-house of Śivajñāna. Accept the position of Ācārya and fulfil my desire. ।।३१॥

तस्येति वचनं श्रुत्वा राक्षसेन्द्रस्य धीमतः। तथेति प्रतिशुश्राव सर्वज्ञो गणनायकः ।।३२।।
Hearing these words of the wise Lord of the Rākṣasas, the omniscient Lord of the Gaṇas promised, saying, “So be it.” ।।३२॥

ततः सन्तुष्टचित्तस्य पौलस्त्यस्येष्टसिद्धये। कोटित्रयं तु लिङ्गानां यथाशास्त्रं यथाविधि।। त्रिकोट्याचार्यरूपेण स्थापितं तेन तत्क्षणे ।। ३३।।
Then, for the fulfilment of the desire of the contented-minded descendant of Pulastya, Reṇuka, assuming the form of three crore Ācāryas, immediately installed three crores of Śivaliṅgas according to the Śāstras and prescribed rites. ।।३३॥

तादृशं तस्य माहात्म्यं समालोक्य विभीषणः। प्रणनाम मुहुर्भक्त्या पादयोस्तस्य विस्मितः ।।३४।।
Beholding such greatness of Reṇukācārya, Vibhīṣaṇa was astonished and repeatedly bowed down at his feet with devotion. ।।३४॥

प्रणतं विनयोपेतं प्रहृष्टं राक्षसेश्वरम्। अनुगृह्य स्वमाहात्म्याद् रेणुकोऽन्तर्हितोऽभवत् ।। ३५।।
Having bestowed his grace upon the Lord of the Rākṣasas, who was humble, delighted, and bowed before him, Reṇuka disappeared by his own divine power. ।।३५॥

विभीषणोऽपि हृष्टात्मा रेणुकस्य प्रसादतः । शिवभक्तिरसासक्तः स्थिरराज्यमपालयत् ।। ३६।।
By the grace of Reṇukācārya, Vibhīṣaṇa too, with a joyful heart and immersed in the bliss of devotion to Śiva, continued to rule and protect his kingdom firmly. ।।३६॥

रेणुकोऽपि महातेजाः सञ्चरन् क्षितिमण्डले । प्रच्छन्नश्च प्रकाशख परमाद्वैतभावितः ।। ३७।।
The greatly radiant Reṇuka also wandered throughout the earth, sometimes concealed and sometimes manifest, absorbed in the supreme non-dual state.

कांश्चिद् दृष्टिनिपातेन करुणारसवर्षिणा। अपरानुपदेशेन शिवाद्वैताभिमर्शिना ।। ३८।।
He blessed some through a compassionate glance showering the nectar of grace, and instructed others in the contemplation and understanding of Śivādvaita.

अन्यांश्च सहवासेन समस्तमलहारिणा। कृतार्थयन् जनान् सर्वान् कृतिनः पक्वकर्मिणः ।।३९ ।।
He fulfilled the lives of others through his association, which removed all impurities, making all virtuous people whose karmas had ripened spiritually fulfilled.

दर्शयित्वा निजाधिक्यं शिवदर्शनलालसः । खण्डयित्वा दुराचारान् पाषण्डान् भिन्नदर्शनान् ।।४० ।।
Desiring to establish the vision of Śiva, he demonstrated his own supremacy, refuted the ill-conducted and heretical followers of divergent doctrines,

यन्त्रमन्त्रकलासिद्धान् विमतान् सिद्धमण्डलान्। विजित्य स्वप्रभावेण स्थापयित्वा शिवागमान्।। आजगाम निजावासं कोल्लिपाक्यभिधं पुरम् ।।४१।।
conquered through his own power the assemblies of those who had attained mastery over yantras, mantras, and various arts, established the Śaivāgamas, and then returned to his own abode, the city known as Kollipāka. ।।४१॥


तत्र सम्भावितः सर्वैर्जनैः शिवपरायणैः । सोमनाथाभिधानस्य शिवस्य प्राप मन्दिरम् ।।४२।।
There, having been honored by all the people devoted to Śiva, Reṇukācārya went to the temple of Śiva known as Somanātha. ।।४२॥

पश्यतां तत्र सर्वेषां भक्तानां शिवयोगिनाम्। तन्वानो विस्मयं भावैस्तुष्टाव परमेश्वरम् ।।४३।।
There, in the presence of all the Śivayogī devotees who were watching, he increased their wonder through his devotional expressions and praised the Supreme Lord. ।।४३॥

देव देव जगनाथ जगत्कारणकारण। ब्रह्मविष्णुसुराधीशवन्द्यमानपदाम्बुज
O God of gods, Lord of the universe, cause of the causes of the world! Your lotus feet are worshipped by Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and the Lord of the gods, Indra. ।।४४॥

संसारनाटकभ्रान्तिकलानिर्वहणप्रद समस्तवेदवेदान्तपरिबोधितवैभव 1 ।।४५।।
You are the one who brings about the performance of the art of the delusion that is the drama of worldly existence, and whose glory is fully revealed by all the Vedas and Vedānta. ।।४५॥

संसारवैद्य सर्वज्ञ सर्वशक्तिनिरङ्कुश । सच्चिदानन्द सर्वस्व परमाकाशविग्रह।।४६ ।।
You are the physician who cures the disease of worldly existence, omniscient, omnipotent, unrestricted, the very essence of Existence, Consciousness, and Bliss, and whose form is the supreme space. ।।४६॥

समस्तजगदाधार ज्योतिर्लिङ्गविजृम्भण सदाशिवमुखानेकदिव्यमूर्तिकलाधर 1 ।।४७।।
You are the support of the entire universe, the one who manifests the Jyotirliṅga, and the bearer of the various divine forms beginning with Sadāśiva. ।।४७॥

गुणत्रयपदातीत मलत्रयविनाशन। जगत्त्रयविलासात्मन् श्रुतित्रयविलोचन ।।४८।।
You are beyond the realm of the three guṇas, the destroyer of the three impurities, the very essence of the divine play of the three worlds, and the one whose three eyes are the three Vedas—Ṛg, Yajur, and Sāma. ।।४८॥

पाहि मां परमेशान पाहि मां पार्वतीपते। त्वदाज्ञया मयैतावत्कालमात्रं महीतले ।। अचारि भवदुक्तानामागमानां प्रसिद्धये ।। ४९।।
Protect me, O Supreme Lord! Protect me, O Lord of Pārvatī! By your command, I have wandered upon this earth all this time for the propagation and dissemination of the Āgamas spoken by you. ।।४९॥

अतः परं स्वरूपं ते प्राप्तुकामोऽस्मि शङ्कर। अन्तरं देहि मे किञ्चिद्नुकम्पाविशेषतः ।।५०।।
O Śaṅkara, henceforth I desire to attain your true nature. Out of your special compassion, grant me a little time and opportunity for this. ।।५०॥

इत्युक्ते गणनायकेन सहसा लिङ्गात् ततः शाङ्करात् वत्सागच्छ महानुभाव भवतो भक्त्या प्रसन्नोऽस्म्यहम्। इत्युच्चैरगदाद् वचस्तनुभृतामाश्चर्यमासीत्तदा दिव्यो दुन्दुभिराननाद गगने पुष्पं ववर्षुर्गणाः ।।५१ ।।
When the Lord of the Gaṇas had spoken thus, suddenly a voice arose loudly from that Śāṅkara Liṅga: “Come, O child! O great soul! I am pleased with your devotion.” At that time, all embodied beings were filled with wonder. A divine drum resounded in the sky, and the Gaṇas showered flowers. ।।५१॥

श्रुत्वा लिङ्गाद् वचनमुदितं शाङ्करं सानुकम्पं संहृष्टात्मा गणपतिरथो ज्योतिषा दीप्यमानः। जातोत्कण्ठैः परमनुचरैर्योगिभिः स्तूयमानो ज्योतिर्लिङ्ग परमनुविशत् स्वप्रकाशं तदानीम् ।।५ २ ।।
Hearing the compassionate words of Śaṅkara that had emerged from the Liṅga, the Lord of the Gaṇas became exceedingly delighted. He began to shine with divine radiance. At that moment, being praised by the greatly eager Yogis who were his supreme attendants, he entered into the supreme, self-luminous Jyotirliṅga. ।।५२॥

लीने तस्मिन् शाङ्करे स्वप्रकाशे दिव्याकारे रेणुके सिद्धनाथे। सर्वो लोको विस्मिताभूत्तदानीं शैवी भक्तिः सप्रमाणा बभूव ।।५३ ।।
When the Siddhanātha Reṇukācārya, of divine form, had merged into that self-luminous Śāṅkara Liṅga, all the people were filled with wonder, and at that time the Śaivī devotion was established as authentic. ।।५३॥

श्रीवेदागमवीरशैवसरणिं श्रीजीवेश्वरयोगपद्यतरणिं श्रीषट्स्थलोद्यन्मणिं श्रीगोप्यचिन्तामणिम् । श्रीसिद्धान्तशिखामणिं लिखयिता यस्तं लिखित्वा परान् श्रुत्वा श्रावयिता स याति विमलां भुक्तिं च मुक्तिं पराम् ।।५४।।
The Śrī Siddhāntaśikhāmaṇi, which follows the path of the Vedic and Āgamic Vīraśaiva doctrine, which is the radiant jewel of the six auspicious Sthalas, which is the sun to the lotus of the union of Jīva and Īśvara, and which is the secret Cintāmaṇi—whoever writes it, causes it to be written, writes it and recites it to others, or hears it himself, attains pure enjoyment and supreme liberation. ।।५४॥

ॐ तत्सत् इति श्रीशिवगीतेषु सिद्धान्तागमेषु शिवाद्वैतविद्यायां शिवयोगशास्त्रे श्रीरेणुकागस्त्यसंवादे वीरशैवधर्मनिर्णये श्रीशिवयोगिशिवाचार्यविरचिते श्रीसिद्धान्तशिखामणौ विभीषणाभीष्टवरप्रदानप्रसङ्गो नाम एकविंशतितमः परिच्छेदः ।
Thus, the twenty-first chapter of the Śrī Siddhāntaśikhāmaṇi, composed by Śrī Śivayogī Śivācārya, concerning the granting of Vibhīṣaṇa’s desired boon in the dialogue between Śrī Reṇuka and Agastya, within the Śivādvaita Vidyā of the Siddhānta Āgamas of the Śrī Śivagītās and the Śivayogaśāstra, in the determination of the Vīraśaiva Dharma, is concluded.

इस प्रकार श्रीसिद्धान्तशिखामणि के रेणुकविभीषणसम्वाद तथा रेणुकशिवलिङ्गक्यप्रसङ्ग नामक एकविंश परिच्छेद की आचार्य राधेश्यामचतुर्वेदीकृत 'ज्ञानवती' हिन्दी व्याख्या पूर्ण हुई ।। २१ ।।
Thus concludes the Hindi commentary ‘Jñānavatī’ by Ācārya Rādheśyāma Caturvedī on the twenty-first chapter of the Śrī Siddhāntaśikhāmaṇi, entitled “The dialogue between Reṇuka and Vibhīṣaṇa and the account of Reṇuka’s union with the Śivaliṅga.” ।।२१।।

।। श्रीसिद्धान्तशिखामणी ग्रंथ समाप्त ।।
।। The Śrī Siddhāntaśikhāmaṇi text is hereby concluded. ।।
